looking at them and nodding, but then I realize I missed half of what they said and have to ask them to go over it again like I wasn’t even there.\n- key: S005\n  name: Organization_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty organizing tasks and activities.\n  manifestation: Since I’ve been stuck doing the shop paperwork, I can’t seem to figure out what needs doing first, so bids, schedules, invoices, and apprentice questions all pile up on my bench until I’m rushing and still missing deadlines.\n- key: S007\n  name: Losing_Items\n  description: Often loses things necessary for tasks or activities.\n  manifestation: I’m constantly hunting for my keys, tape measure, phone, order sheets, or the notes I just had in my hand, and it eats up so much of the day that the guys have started checking behind me.\n- key: S008\n  name: Distractibility\n  description: Often easily distracted by extraneous stimuli (for older adolescents and adults, may include unrelated thoughts).\n  manifestation: What brings me in is I cannot keep my mind on one thing anymore—one saw starting up, somebody walking past the office, or even a thought about another job will pull me off what I’m doing, and then the whole day gets away from me.\n- key: S012\n  name: Feeling_Restless\n  description: Often runs about or climbs in situations where it is inappropriate. (Note: In adolescents or adults, may be limited to feeling restless.)\n  manifestation: If I have to sit at the desk doing estimates or sit through a long meeting, I feel wound up inside like I need to get up and move around, even though my wrist is healed and I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n- key: S013\n  name: Quiet_Activity_Difficulty\n  description: Often unable to play or engage in leisure activities quietly.\n  manifestation: Even at home or in church, when things are supposed to be quiet, I catch myself tapping, shifting around, whispering to my husband, or getting up for no real reason because sitting still in the quiet feels almost unbearable.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Even back in grade school, before I was 12, teachers were always telling my mother I was bright but careless, losing homework, forgetting instructions, and staring out the window instead of finishing my seatwork.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: My mistakes and delays have cost me commissions, caused rework on jobs, and made the crew less willing to rely on me for scheduling or ordering. At home, my husband has had to take over bills and reminders because I keep misplacing papers or missing what needs to be done.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 months\n  manifestation: This level of trouble has been going on for about a year and a month, since I broke my wrist and got shifted into shop paperwork and supervising instead of hands-on carpentry.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 57 / carpenter / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S008\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety, like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. If I’m stuck at the desk doing estimates, or sitting through a long meeting, I get this wound-up feeling inside, like I need to stand up and move around. Even when there’s no reason for it and I know I should just sit there and finish, it’s hard to stay put.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think of myself as doing things without thinking, not in a big way. I might speak too quickly sometimes, but I can’t say that’s a main problem I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not often. I might jump in now and then if I’m trying to make a point, but I haven’t really noticed interrupting people as a big problem for me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often have trouble completing tasks or projects?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Yes. At work it’s been a real problem. I’ve made mistakes or gotten behind on things, and it’s cost me commissions. I mean, I had some issues when I was younger, but the really bad trouble has been going on about a year and a month now, since I broke my wrist and got moved into more shop paperwork and supervising instead of doing the hands-on carpentry.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with attention and restlessness caused problems for you in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes. At work it’s been a real problem. I’ve made mistakes or gotten behind on things, and it’s cost me commissions. Sometimes jobs have had to be redone because I missed something or didn’t get it handled in time. The crew doesn’t really trust me as much now with scheduling or ordering materials, and that feels awful.\n\nAt home, my husband has had to take over the bills and remind me about things, because I keep losing papers or missing what needs to be done. So yes, it’s definitely affecting both.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overwhelmed, anxious, or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I do feel overwhelmed sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious or depressed. description: Often has difficulty organizing tasks and activities.\n  manifestation: Since I’ve been stuck doing the shop paperwork, I can’t seem to figure out what needs doing first, so bids, schedules, invoices, and apprentice questions all pile up on my bench until I’m rushing and still missing deadlines.\n- key: S007\n  name: Losing_Items\n  description: Often loses things necessary for tasks or activities.\n  manifestation: I’m constantly hunting for my keys, tape measure, phone, order sheets, or the notes I just had in my hand, and it eats up so much of the day that the guys have started checking behind me.\n- key: S008\n  name: Distractibility\n  description: Often easily distracted by extraneous stimuli (for older adolescents and adults, may include unrelated thoughts).\n  manifestation: What brings me in is I cannot keep my mind on one thing anymore—one saw starting up, somebody walking past the office, or even a thought about another job will pull me off what I’m doing, and then the whole day gets away from me.\n- key: S012\n  name: Feeling_Restless\n  description: Often runs about or climbs in situations where it is inappropriate. (Note: In adolescents or adults, may be limited to feeling restless.)\n  manifestation: If I have to sit at the desk doing estimates or sit through a long meeting, I feel wound up inside like I need to get up and move around, even though my wrist is healed and I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n- key: S013\n  name: Quiet_Activity_Difficulty\n  description: Often unable to play or engage in leisure activities quietly.\n  manifestation: Even at home or in church, when things are supposed to be quiet, I catch myself tapping, shifting around, whispering to my husband, or getting up for no real reason because sitting still in the quiet feels almost unbearable.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Even back in grade school, before I was 12, teachers were always telling my mother I was bright but careless, losing homework, forgetting instructions, and staring out the window instead of finishing my seatwork.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: My mistakes and delays have cost me commissions, caused rework on jobs, and made the crew less willing to rely on me for scheduling or ordering. At home, my husband has had to take over bills and reminders because I keep misplacing papers or missing what needs to be done.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 months\n  manifestation: This level of trouble has been going on for about a year and a month, since I broke my wrist and got shifted into shop paperwork and supervising instead of hands-on carpentry.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 57 / carpenter / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S008\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with attention and restlessness caused problems for you in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes. At work it’s been a real problem. I’ve made mistakes or gotten behind on things, and it’s cost me commissions. Sometimes jobs have had to be redone because I missed something or didn’t get it handled in time. The crew doesn’t really trust me as much now with scheduling or ordering materials, and that feels awful.\n\nAt home, my husband has had to take over the bills and remind me about things, because I keep losing papers or missing what needs to be done. So yes, it’s definitely affecting both.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overwhelmed, anxious, or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I do feel overwhelmed sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious or depressed. It’s more like I get frustrated and worn out by trying to keep up.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with paying attention, following instructions, or being restless when you were a teenager?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. I’d lose homework, forget what I was supposed to do, or just be staring out the window instead of finishing my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be physically active, like running, climbing, or engaging in other intense physical activities, now that you're an adult?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Not so much running or climbing or anything like that, but if I have to sit at the desk doing estimates, or sit through a long meeting, I get really wound up inside. I feel like I need to get up and move around, even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with attention and restlessness your whole life, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s gotten to this level more recently. I mean, I had some issues when I was younger, but the really bad trouble has been going on about a year and a month now, since I broke my wrist and got moved into more shop paperwork and supervising instead of doing the hands-on carpentry.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with attention and restlessness caused problems for you in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes. At work it’s been a real problem. I’ve made mistakes or gotten behind on things, and it’s cost me commissions. The crew doesn’t really trust me as much now with scheduling or ordering materials, and that feels awful.\n\nAt home, my husband has had to take over the bills and remind me about things, because I keep losing papers or missing what needs to be done. So yes, it’s definitely affecting both.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overwhelmed, anxious, or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I do feel overwhelmed sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious or depressed. It’s more like I get frustrated and worn out by trying to keep up.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with paying attention, following instructions, or being restless when you were a teenager?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. Even before I was a teenager, like in grade school, my teachers would tell my mother I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget what instructions were, or just sit there staring out the window instead of finishing my work. So it wasn’t totally new, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting for something?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed that as a big problem for me. I might get impatient sometimes like anybody, but I can’t think of it being something that causes trouble.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming so focused on a task that you lose track of everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I can get into something if I’m working on it, but I don’t know if I’d say I lose track of everything around me. I haven’t really noticed that as a problem.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with attention or restlessness that you remember being noticed by your parents or teachers before you were 7 years old?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I know I’ve said some of this, but even in grade school my teachers would tell my mother I was bright, but careless. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I just can’t keep my mind on one thing anymore. At work, if a saw starts up, or somebody walks past the office, or even if I suddenly think about another job I need to do, I’m off track. Then I lose where I was, and before I know it the whole day’s gotten away from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety, like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. If I’m stuck at the desk doing estimates, or sitting through a long meeting, I get this wound-up feeling inside, like I need to stand up and move around. Even when there’s no reason for it and I know I should just sit there and finish, it’s hard to stay put.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think of myself as doing things without thinking, not in a big way. I might speak too quickly sometimes, but I can’t say that’s a main problem I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not often. I might jump in now and then if I’m trying to make a point, but I haven’t really noticed interrupting people as a big problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects?\n\nPatient: Yes. Since I’ve been stuck doing more of the shop paperwork, it’s gotten pretty bad. I have bids, schedules, invoices, and the apprentices asking me things, and I can’t seem to sort out what needs to come first. It all piles up on my bench, and then I’m rushing at the last minute and still missing deadlines.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your time or tasks?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. I have a hard time figuring out what needs to come first. With all the shop paperwork now—bids, schedules, invoices—and then the apprentices asking me things, it just piles up on my bench. I don’t remember being especially restless as a kid, not in a way that stands out to me. I’d lose homework, forget what I was supposed to do, or just be staring out the window instead of finishing my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be physically active, like running, climbing, or engaging in other intense physical activities, now that you're an adult?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Not so much running or climbing or anything like that, but if I have to sit at the desk doing estimates, or sit through a long meeting, I get really wound up inside. I feel like I need to get up and move around, even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with attention and restlessness your whole life, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s gotten to this level more recently. I mean, I had some issues when I was younger, but the really bad trouble has been going on about a year and a month now, since I broke my wrist and got moved into more shop paperwork and supervising instead of doing the hands-on carpentry.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with attention and restlessness caused problems for you in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes. So yes, it’s definitely affecting both.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overwhelmed, anxious, or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I do feel overwhelmed sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious or depressed. It’s more like I get frustrated and worn out by trying to keep up.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with paying attention, following instructions, or being restless when you were a teenager?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. Even before I was a teenager, like in grade school, my teachers would tell my mother I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget what instructions were, or just sit there staring out the window instead of finishing my work. So it wasn’t totally new, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting for something?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed that as a big problem for me. I might get impatient sometimes like anybody, but I can’t think of it being something that causes trouble.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming so focused on a task that you lose track of everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I can get into something if I’m working on it, but I don’t know if I’d say I lose track of everything around me. I haven’t really noticed that as a problem.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with attention or restlessness that you remember being noticed by your parents or teachers before you were 7 years old?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I know I’ve said some of this, but even in grade school my teachers would tell my mother I was bright, but careless. I’d lose homework, forget instructions, and instead of finishing my seatwork I’d be staring out the window. I’m not sure about before exactly age 7, but it was definitely early on.\n\nDoctor: Did your parents ever mention to you that you had trouble sitting still or paying attention when you were very young, like before you started school?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about before I started school exactly. What I do remember is that by grade school, definitely before I was 12, teachers were telling my mother I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget instructions, and sit there staring out the window instead of finishing my work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. So yes, it’s definitely affecting both.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overwhelmed, anxious, or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I do feel overwhelmed sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious or depressed. It’s more like I get frustrated and worn out by trying to keep up.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with paying attention, following instructions, or being restless when you were a teenager?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. Even before I was a teenager, like in grade school, my teachers would tell my mother I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget what instructions were, or just sit there staring out the window instead of finishing my work. So it wasn’t totally new, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting for something?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed that as a big problem for me. I might get impatient sometimes like anybody, but I can’t think of it being something that causes trouble.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming so focused on a task that you lose track of everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I can get into something if I’m working on it, but I don’t know if I’d say I lose track of everything around me. I haven’t really noticed that as a problem.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with attention or restlessness that you remember being noticed by your parents or teachers before you were 7 years old?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I know I’ve said some of this, but even in grade school my teachers would tell my mother I was bright, but careless. I’d lose homework, forget instructions, and instead of finishing my seatwork I’d be staring out the window. I’m not sure about before exactly age 7, but it was definitely early on.\n\nDoctor: Did your parents ever mention to you that you had trouble sitting still or paying attention when you were very young, like before you started school?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about before I started school exactly. What I do remember is that by grade school, definitely before I was 12, teachers were telling my mother I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget instructions, and sit there staring out the window instead of finishing my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever become so focused on something that you ignore everything else around you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can focus if I’m into something, but I don’t think I tune everything out or ignore what’s going on around me. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I might jump in now and then if I’m trying to make a point, but I haven’t really noticed interrupting people as a big problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects?\n\nPatient: Yes. Since I’ve been stuck doing more of the shop paperwork, it’s gotten pretty bad. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I just can’t keep my mind on one thing anymore. At work, if a saw starts up, or somebody walks past the office, or even if I suddenly think about another job I need to do, I’m off track. Then I lose where I was, and before I know it the whole day’s gotten away from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety, like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. If I’m stuck at the desk doing estimates, or sitting through a long meeting, I get this wound-up feeling inside, like I need to stand up and move around. Even when there’s no reason for it and I know I should just sit there and finish, it’s hard to stay put.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think of myself as doing things without thinking, not in a big way. I might speak too quickly sometimes, but I can’t say that’s a main problem I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not often. I might jump in now and then if I’m trying to make a point, but I haven’t really noticed interrupting people as a big problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects?\n\nPatient: Yes. Since I’ve been stuck doing more of the shop paperwork, it’s gotten pretty bad. I have bids, schedules, invoices, and the apprentices asking me things, and I can’t seem to sort out what needs to come first. It all piles up on my bench, and then I’m rushing at the last minute and still missing deadlines.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your time or tasks?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. I have a hard time figuring out what needs to come first. With all the shop paperwork now—bids, schedules, invoices—and then the apprentices asking me things, it just piles up on my bench. Then I’m rushing around at the last minute and still missing deadlines.\n\nDoctor: Were you very physically active or restless when you were a child?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t remember being especially restless as a kid, not in a way that stands out to me. My memory of that is kind of fuzzy.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or following instructions when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. Even in grade school, before I was 12, teachers would tell my mother I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget what I was supposed to do, or just be staring out the window instead of finishing my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be physically active, like running, climbing, or engaging in other intense physical activities, now that you're an adult?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Not so much running or climbing or anything like that, but if I have to sit at the desk doing estimates, or sit through a long meeting, I get really wound up inside. I feel like I need to get up and move around, even when I know I’m supposed to just stay put and finish.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with attention and restlessness your whole life, or did they start more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s gotten to this level more recently. I mean, I had some issues when I was younger, but the really bad trouble has been going on about a year and a month now, since I broke my wrist and got moved into more shop paperwork and supervising instead of doing the hands-on carpentry.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with attention and restlessness caused problems for you in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes. At work it’s been a real problem. I’ve made mistakes or gotten behind on things, and it’s cost me commissions. Sometimes jobs have had to be redone because I missed something or didn’t get it handled in time. The crew doesn’t really trust me as much now with scheduling or ordering materials, and that feels awful.\n\nAt home, my husband has had to take over the bills and remind me about things, because I keep losing papers or missing what needs to be done. So yes, it’s definitely affecting both.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overwhelmed, anxious, or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I do feel overwhelmed sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious or depressed. It’s more like I get frustrated and worn out by trying to keep up.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with paying attention, following instructions, or being restless when you were a teenager?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. Even before I was a teenager, like in grade school, my teachers would tell my mother I was bright but careless. I’d lose homework, forget what instructions were, or just sit there staring out the window instead of finishing my work. So it wasn’t totally new, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting for something?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed that as a big problem for me. I might get impatient sometimes like anybody, but I can’t think of it being something that causes trouble.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming so focused on a task that you lose track of everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t really noticed that as a problem.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with attention or restlessness that you remember being noticed by your parents or teachers before you were 7 years old?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so.
